Growth  
with Discipline 
Over the years, we have grown responsibly by 
consistently expanding our manufacturing network, 
diversifying our portfolio, deepening our distribution 
reach, and building brands that customers trust�  
The careful thought behind these decisions and 
their disciplined execution have well positioned our 
business for the future�
FY26 marks an important stage in this evolution� The disciplined 
investments we have made across manufacturing, product development, 
distribution, and brand-building are now translating into stronger market 
positions and new avenues of growth� While our established businesses 
continue to reinforce Astral's leadership, newer categories are rapidly 
gaining scale, contributing to a highly balanced and resilient portfolio�
This year also reflects a broader ambition� Through Vision 2050, Astral 
is building capabilities that extend beyond our traditional strengths, 
creating an integrated building materials platform for the evolving 
needs of construction, infrastructure, and water management� Targeted 
investments in advanced manufacturing, specialised products, and 
backward integration are driving competitive advancement while 
preparing the business for the opportunities of the decades ahead�
Our footprint continues to expand, guided by this same measured 
approach� Improved operational performance in the United Kingdom, 
growing capabilities in the United States, and the establishment of 
operations in the UAE demonstrate a highly calculated strategy for 
building an international business� Across domestic markets, impactful 
consumer campaigns, stronger brand investments, and deeper rural 
penetration have enhanced our visibility and reinforced customer 
confidence across our entire portfolio�
The journey forward is governed by the exact 
principles that have shaped Astral from the 
beginning: careful execution, disciplined 
investment, and a willingness to innovate with 
purpose� These principles ensure we grow 
consistently, adapt confidently, and create lasting 
value for all our stakeholders�Key Performance Highlights-

Astral manages 21 
manufacturing units located in 
India, the United Kingdom, and 
the United States, with a total 
production capacity of 5.97 lakh 
metric tonnes per year. Over 
the years, Astral has invested in 
expanding and decentralising 
its manufacturing footprint, 
enabling greater operational 
efficiency, faster servicing of 
regional markets and improved 
supply chain resilience. 
Astral’s products reach customers through 
one of the most extensive distribution 
networks in the industry, comprising over 
3,990 distributors and more than 2.7 lakh 
dealers across the country. This strong 
market presence is complemented by a 
growing international footprint, with the 
products reaching customers in more than 
40 countries. Building for  
What’s NextABOUT THE COMPANY
Astral Limited, founded in 1996 as a piping solutions business in Indian markets, has 
expanded beyond its original business, building capabilities across multiple categories that 
touch every stage of construction and infrastructure lifecycle from pipes and fittings, the 
company’s portfolio has grown to include water tanks, adhesive and sealants, construction 
chemicals, paints, bathware, specialised valves and infrastructure solutions �

Building 
Portfolio for 
GrowthPRODUCT PORTFOLIO
Astral’s diversified product portfolio 
is designed to address the evolving 
requirements of modern construction, 
infrastructure and urban development �  
From water management and piping systems 
to adhesives, construction chemicals, paints 
and bathware, the company’s offerings 
deliver performance, durability and reliability 
across residential, commercial, industrial and 
infrastructure applications �
Astral continues to drive innovation and customer 
centric product development while expanding its 
portfolio with specialised and value-added solutions 
that address emerging industry needs.Astral offers one of India’s most comprehensive ranges of 
plastic piping solutions, catering to plumbing, drainage, 
agriculture, industrial infrastructure and fire protection 
applications. The portfolio combines advanced technologies, 
superior material performance and application specific 
solutions.
• Plumbing Pipes & Fittings
• Sewerage & Drainage 
Pipes & fittings
• Agriculture Pipes & 
Fittings
• Cable Protection System 
• Industrial Piping System 
• Fire Protection System 
• Urban Infrastructure 
Solutions
• Thermostatic Ball Valves
• Cable Protection Systems
• Column Pipes
• Conduit Pipe
• Ancilliary ProductsPipes and Fittings
• OPVC Pipes
• Silencio Low-noise Drainage 
System
• DrainPro Drainage Solutions
• PEX Composite Piping System
Astral’s water tanks provide safe, hygienic, and durable water 
storage solutions for residential, commerical and Industrial 
applications. Incorporating advanced features and certified 
standards, our tanks reflect a commitment to quality and 
innovation in every detail. Our water tanks come in 4 Layers and 
3 Layers with capacities ranging from 500 liters to 10,000 liters.
• Roto Moulding Water 
Tanks - 4 layers, 3 Layers 
and 2 Layers

3 Layers
Astral offers an extensive portfolio of adhesives and sealants 
for construction, maintenance, woodworking, and industrial 
use. Our solutions are recognised for their consistent quality, 
robust performance, and reliability across a wide range of 
applications.

advanced waterproofing, tiling, and grouting needs. These

longevity of construction projects.

Transformation  
in MotionFROM THE CHAIRMAN'S DESK
Dear Shareholders,
As we complete two decades as a listed company,  
we look back and reflect on how far we have come 
and the future that we are building. From a ₹ 50 crore 
enterprise focused on a single product category, Astral 
has today evolved into a diversified company with 
presence across pipes, fittings, adhesives, construction 
chemicals, bathware, paints, water storage solutions and 
specialised infrastructure products. This transformation 
has been guided by a commitment to innovation, 
customer centricity and long-term value creation.wastewater systems and specialised 
infrastructure applications. A 
significant step in this direction 
has been our growing focus on 
advanced polymer technologies 
and specialised applications. The 
integration of Al-Aziz has further 
enhanced our capabilities in 
specialised fittings and polyolefin-
based solutions, opening new 
opportunities across utility, 
infrastructure and industrial 
applications. 
Progress Across 
Businesses
FY 26 witnessed encouraging 
progress across Astral’s diversified 
portfolio. 
Our plumbing business continued 
reinforcing its market leadership 
through product innovation, 
manufacturing expansion and 
growing adoption of specialised 
solutions across residential, 
commercial and infrastructure 
applications. Products such as 
our Power Drain surface drainage 
systems are now being deployed 
in landmark projects, including the 
Narendra Modi stadium and the 
Dharamshala stadium.
Astral continued to strengthen 
its Fire Pro portfolio during FY26, 
supported by growing acceptance 
of UL/FM-approved fire sprinkler 
piping systems. This business 
recorded a significant increase in 
sales during the year, reflecting 
increasing adoption of certified 
fire protection solutions across 
commercial applications. We also 
continued to expand our premium 
drainage portfolio through Silencio, 
our low-noise drainage system 
designed for modern buildings. 
During the year, we expanded the 
product range and continued to 
position Silencio as a differentiated 
solution within our value-added 
plumbing portfolio.
The adhesive business continued 
to deepen its market presence 
through product innovation, 
distribution network expansion 
and focused brand building 
initiatives. During the year, Bondtite 
entered a new phase of growth 
through nationwide consumer 
campaigns involving Ranbir Kapoor 
helping enhance brand visibility 
and engagement across key markets. We also put in efforts to 
improve rural penetration. Today, 
our network reaches over 13,000 
towns across India, with a clear 
roadmap to reach 20,000 towns 
in the next few years. The recently 
launched New Bharat initiative is 
helping accelerate rural market 
development and creating new 
growth opportunities across 
emerging consumption centres.
The paints business also continued 
to make steady gains through 
portfolio enhancements, market 
expansion and distribution 
development. Building on the 
strength of both Astral Paints and 
Gem Paints, we continue laying 
foundation for sustainable long-
term growth. We have entered into 
6 states and shortly we are planning 
to add more states, with an aim of 
eventually  becoming a Pan-India 
company.
Our international adhesive business 
also demonstrated encouraging 
progress during the year. In 
the United Kingdom, focused 
restructuring efforts, improved 
operational discipline and the 
integration of advanced hybrid 
sealant technology acquired 
from Switzerland have helped 
improve business performance 
and profitability. In the United 
States, we continue to leverage 
our manufacturing presence 
and product capabilities to serve 
niche industrial and specialised 
application. We established local 
office in the UAE, to further enhance 
Astral's global footprint in plumbing 
and adhesive business.
Bathware emerged as one of our 
most promising growth platforms 
during the year. We expanded our 
distribution footprint through the 
addition of 80 new distributors. The 
introduction of three new premium 
product ranges led to 25%+ channel 
partner growth. Growing traction 
across both retail and project 
segments has resulted in a project 
pipeline increasing very fast, 
reinforcing our confidence in the 
long-term potential of this business.
Across businesses, our focus 
remains on delivering solutions to 
serve the customer better. Whether 
through advanced drainage 
systems, fire protection solutions, 
waterproofing technologies or specialised building materials, 
we seek to address customer 
challenges through innovation, 
performance and reliability. This 
approach enables us to build 
deeper customer relationships as 
we build on our position in higher 
value segments
Sustainability as Growth 
Imperative
Sustainability is becoming an 
integral part of how we operate, 
innovate and grow. We recognise 
that long term value creation 
must be aligned with responsible 
environmental stewardship and 
efficient resource utilisation. During 
FY 26, we expanded our renewable 
energy initiatives, generating 7 MW 
from solar and wind energy sources 
and increased our renewable 
energy usage by 39%. We advanced 
our digital ESG transformation 
journey to enhance data collection, 
monitoring and reporting 
capabilities. Our commitment to 
sustainable product innovation was 
further reinforced through GRIHA 
certification for products including 
Aquasafe, Silencio, Aquarius, 
Drain Pro and Drainmaster. We 
will continue to invest in solutions 
that contribute to more efficient, 
sustainable and resilient built 
environments while creating long 
term value for stakeholders. Our 
ESG score has improved to 60 
from 48 during the current year 
against industry average of 34.
Building Long-term 
Strategic Advantage
One of the most significant 
initiatives underway is the 
development of our captive 
CPVC resin manufacturing 
project through Nexelon Chem. 
Astral will be the only CPVC pipe 
manufacturer in India to produce 
its own resin. The captive 40,000 
MT will reduce import dependence, 
currency exposure, and is expected 
to improve volumes and margins on 
CPVC pipes and fittings from FY28. 
This project represents a strategic 
step towards greater self-reliance, 
supply chain resilience and long-
term value creation. 
Similarly, investments in product 
authentication technologies, 
advanced manufacturing capabilities and specialised 
product platforms are helping 
create durable competitive 
advantages that extend beyond 
traditional market boundaries.
Our integrated business model 
remains another important 
differentiator. By leveraging 
common distribution ecosystem 
across multiple product categories, 
Astral is able to create meaningful 
synergies, deepen customer 
engagement and expand its share 
of wallet across construction value 
chain. This ability to scale new 
business through an established 
platform will continue to be a key 
driver of growth in the years ahead. 
Looking Ahead
India’s long term growth outlook 
remains compelling. Urbanisation, 
infrastructure development, 
housing demand, industrial 
expansion, and rising aspirations 
continue to create significant 
opportunities across building 
materials sector. Supported by a 
trusted brand, expanding product 
portfolio, robust manufacturing 
footprint, distribution network 
and team of 9,500 dedicated 
employees, Astral is well positioned 
to capitalise on these opportunities 
through investing in innovation, 
strengthening our market 
presence, advancing sustainability 
and creating enduring value for all 
stakeholders.
On behalf of the Board, 
I would like to thank our 
employees, customers, 
distributors, dealers, 
business partners and 
shareholders for their 
continued trust and 
support� Together, we will 
continue building beyond 
boundaries and shaping 
the future of India's 
building materials industry �

Growth 
Anchored 
in DisciplineMESSAGE FROM WHOLE TIME DIRECTOR AND CHIEF FINANCIAL OFFICER
Dear Shareholders,
FY 26 was a year of opportunity as well as volatility.  
It showed the resilience of Astral’s business model and 
the power of its financial foundations. The operating 
environment was characterised by significant fluctuations 
in raw material prices, evolving channel dynamics and 
continued global uncertainties. Against this backdrop, 
Astral remained focused on disciplined execution, 
prudent capital allocation and sustainable value creation. 
During the financial year ending 
March 31, 2026, Astral achieved 
a historic consolidated revenue 
milestone of ₹65,686 million, 
representing 12.6% Year-on-Year 
growth over the previous fiscal. 
Consolidated EBITDA expanded 
to ₹11,092 million, marking a 12.4% 
YoY acceleration. Profit Before Tax 
(PBT) before exceptional items 
climbed to ₹ 7532 million, while 
consolidated Profit After Tax (PAT) 
stood at ₹5347 million. Cash profits 
rose to ₹8,263 million. These 
results, achieved in a challenging 
pricing environment for the most 
part of the year, are a testimony to 
Astral’s disciplined execution. 
Navigating Volatility
The plastics industry experienced 
considerable volatility during 
FY 26, particularly in PVC and 
CPVC resin prices. The industry 
witnessed prolonged periods of 
weak realisations during the initial 
part of the year, followed by sharp 
recovery in raw material prices in 
the later months. enhanced customer service 
levels and reduced logistical 
complexities. At the same time, 
investments in product innovation, 
manufacturing technology and 
category expansion continue to 
create stronger foundation for 
long term growth.
We also continued to invest in 
product innovation, technology 
adoption and category expansion, 
strengthening our ability to 
address evolving customer 
requirements across the building 
materials ecosystem.
Capital Allocation and 
Risk Management
At Astral, risk management is an 
integral part of business. In an 
environment characterised by 
commodity price fluctuations, 
supply chain disruptions and 
evolving market conditions, our 
focus remains on preserving 
operational flexibility while 
safeguarding long term value 
creation. The company’s diversified 
portfolio, broad manufacturing 
footprint and extensive 
distribution network help mitigate 
concentration risk and enhance 
resilience across business cycles. 
Our decentralised manufacturing 
strategy has further improved 
supply chain agility while reducing 
dependence on individual 
geographies and logistics 
corridors.
Equally important is our 
disciplined approach to capital 
allocation. Every major investment 
is evaluated through a long-term 
lens, balancing growth potential 
with financial prudence and risk 
adjusted returns. By adopting this 
approach Astral has managed to 
maintain a strong balance sheet 
and preserve financial flexibility. 
Building Long-term 
Competitive Advantage
One of the most important 
strategic initiatives currently underway is our investment 
in Nexelon Chem, which will 
enable Astral to manufacture 
captive CPVC resin. This project 
will improve supply chain 
resilience through backward 
integration while improving cost 
competitiveness. 
During FY26, the Company also 
maintained a strong focus on 
working capital efficiency, ensuring 
that growth was supported by 
healthy cash generation and 
disciplined resource deployment. 
Our receivable days improved 
and working capital cycle days 
improved from 37 days to 24 days. 
As we move forward, our focus 
will remain on profitable growth, 
disciplined capital allocation, 
prudent risk management and 
the efficient execution of strategic 
investments. We believe these 
priorities will continue to enhance 
our competitive position and 
create sustainable value for all 
stakeholders.
I would like to thank our 
shareholders, customers, 
channel partners, 
lenders, employees and 
all stakeholders for their 
continued trust and 
support� Their confidence 
remains integral to Astral's 
continued success�
Hiranand Savlani

Competitive Advantages 
that Set Us Apart
01
0502
0603
0704
08STRENGTHS 
Astral has achieved sustained growth on the back of a carefully built foundation 
of capabilities that have been built over last three decades � The company has over 
the years navigated evolving market conditions, driven operational excellence and 
created long-term value for stakeholders � 
DIVERSIFIED GROWTH 
PLATFORM
Astral has successfully transformed itself from 
a pipe and fittings company into a diversified 
enterprise spanning nine product categories. 
This evolution has created multiple growth 
engines across plumbing, water management, 
construction chemicals, adhesives, paints and 
bathware. The breadth of the portfolio reduced 
dependence on any single market segment 
while creating opportunities to participate 
across entire construction lifecycle. MANUFACTURING EXCELLENCE
Astral’s manufacturing strategy is built around 
a network of 21 facilities across India, the United 
Kingdom, and the United States. with an 
installed capacity of approximately 5.97 lakh 
metric tonnes per annum. By locating the 
production units near key demand centers, 
Astral has reduced logistics complexity and 
enhanced supply-chain reliability. Recent 
expansions in Hyderabad and Kanpur, along 
with continued development of regional 
facilities have improved service responsiveness 
and supply chain resilience while reducing 
logistics cost. This distributed model enables 
Astral to remain closer to customers while 
supporting scalable growth across multiple 
product categories.PIONEERING SPIRIT AND 
INNOVATION
Innovation has been defining element in Astral’s 
growth journey since inception. During the year, 
the company further expanded its portfolio 
through specialised solutions across plumbing, 
drainage, fire protection and infrastructure 
applications. Products such as Silencio low-noise 
drainage systems, Fire-pro fire protection piping 
solutions, OPVC piping systems and advanced 
drainage technologies continued to gain market 
acceptance. The company also expanded its 
presence across specialised fittings, water 
management and construction chemicals 
reflecting its ability to identify emerging 
opportunities and convert them into scalable 
growth platforms. EXTENSIVE DISTRIBUTION 
NETWORK
Astral’s products are available through a 
network of over 2.7 lakh dealers and more than 
3,990  distributors across India, as well as in 
more than 40 export markets. This vast channel 
ecosystem ensures timely product availability 
and end-to-end service. By leveraging strong 
partner relationships and scalable logistics, 
Astral delivers a seamless experience for 
customers in both core and emerging regions.
STRONG BRAND EQUITY
Astral has built strong brand equity by 
producing innovative quality products as 
well as investing in advertising, promotion 
and brand building activities. In FY 26, the 
company enhanced brand visibility through the 
association with Ranbir Kapoor for Bondtite, 
association of bathware brands with blockbuster 
Dhurandhar and some other targeted 
campaigns that helped deepen customer 
connect and channel engagement. These 
investments are helping Astral gain acceptance 
in newer categories such as paints, bathware 
and construction chemicals. The brand equity 
remains a key enabler of growth across the 
company’s diversified product portfolio. EXECUTION EXCELLENCE
Astral's ability to identify opportunities, scale 
new categories and integrate acquisitions 
has been a key driver of its growth. Whether 
through the successful expansion of adhesives, 
the integration of Gem Paints and Al-Aziz, or 
acquisition of stake in Nexelon Chem or the 
development of new manufacturing facilities, 
the company has consistently demonstrated 
disciplined execution and an ability to translate 
strategic intent into business outcomes.FINANCIAL STRENGTH
A strong balance sheet, disciplined capital 
allocation and healthy cash generation provide 
Astral with the flexibility to invest in growth 
opportunities while maintaining financial 
resilience through business cycles.COMMITMENT TO 
SUSTAINABILITY
In FY26, Astral advanced its environmental, 
social and governance agenda through 
renewable energy installations and the adoption 
of energy-efficiency measures that reduced 
overall carbon intensity. Community-focused 
initiatives under the Astral Foundation further 
demonstrate the Company’s dedication to 
social stewardship and responsible corporate 
citizenship.

Building the 
Next Phase of 
GrowthSTRATEGIC PRIORITIES 
Astral is focused on a set of strategic 
priorities as it embarks on the next 
phase of growth � Through targeted 
investments and disciplined 
execution, Astral is building a 
stronger, future ready organisation � 
NURTURING TALENT AND 
FOSTERING EXCELLENCE
Astral recognises its workforce as the cornerstone 
of sustainable success, with over 9,500 employees 
contributing to operational excellence across 
diverse functions and geographies. The 
Company's comprehensive human resource 
strategy emphasises structured training 
programmes, leadership development, and 
performance enhancement initiatives that 
support individual growth whilst advancing 
organisational objectives. In FY26, Astral 
intensified employee development whilst 
implementing streamlined procedures across 
manufacturing facilities to enhance operational 
efficiency. Through structured development 
pathways, Astral fosters high-performance culture 
that attracts and retains exceptional talent.
MANUFACTURING  
AND DISTRIBUTION
Astral’s strategy included concentrating on 
decentralised manufacturing and increasing 
its distribution reach in more and more towns. 
Astral will be the first company to start PVC 
manufacturing in India through a captive 40,000 
MT plant reducing import dependence, and 
currency exposure. This plant is expected to 
improve volumes and margins on CPVC pipes 
and fittings from FY28. Astral continues to 
expand its distribution footprint across India, with 
presence in over 20,000 towns through a network 
of more than 3990 distributors and more than 
2.7 lakh dealers. This extensive reach enhances 
product availability and customer service.
SCALING EMERGING 
BUSINESSES
Astral continues to work at accelerating the 
growth of its newer business verticals, including 
paints, bathware and construction materials. 
During the year, the company expanded its paint 
business distribution footprint across western 
and southern markets. It launched new products 
in the bathware segment, expanding project 
participation and increasing dealer engagement. 
Astral aims to work at making these businesses 
increasingly meaningful contributors to the 
company’s long-term growth profile.  
EXPANDING PRODUCT 
PORTFOLIO AND DRIVING 
INNOVATION
The Company remains focused on increasing 
the contribution of differentiated and value-
added solutions across its portfolio. Continued 
investments in products such as OPVC pipes, 
FirePro systems, Silencio drainage solutions, 
specialised fittings and infrastructure 
applications reflect Astral's strategy of 
addressing evolving customer requirements 
while strengthening its presence in premium 
segments. EXPANDING GLOBAL 
OPPORTUNITIES
Astral continues to strengthen its international 
presence by leveraging its manufacturing footprint 
across India, the United Kingdom and the United 
States, while exporting to more than 40 countries. 
During FY 26, the company reported strong growth 
in export volumes. Astral also opened an office at 
UAE. The company is also participating in many 
international prestigious shows like ISSH Frankfurt, 
big five Dubai, Big Five Saudi, Big Five South 
Africa. The improving performance of overseas 
operations, coupled with growing acceptance of 
Astral ‘s products across global markets, positions 
the company to diversify revenue streams and build 
stronger international business over long-term.FOSTERING SUSTAINABILITY 
AND CORPORATE 
RESPONSIBILITY
Astral continues to embed sustainability into 
its growth strategy through focused initiatives 
across environmental stewardship, operational 
efficiency and community development. During 
the year, electricity generated from renewable 
sources increased 39% supported by 7MW 
clean energy initiative including solar and wind 
power installations. The Company also advanced 
its digital ESG transformation programmes 
to enhance data collection, monitoring and 
reporting capabilities. Reflecting its commitment 
to sustainable construction, several products, 
including Aquasafe, Silencio, Aquarius, Drain Pro 
and Drainmaster, received GRIHA Green Building 
certification. Astral further strengthened its 
operational excellence framework through 
the implementation of ISO 9001:2015 Quality 
Management Systems at its Cuttack and 
Hyderabad manufacturing facilities. Our ESG 
score has improved to 60 from 48 during the 
current year against industry average of 34.0106

Managing Risks, 
Enabling GrowthRISK MANAGEMENT 
In a rapidly transforming environment, we maintain rigorous vigilance in identifying, 
assessing, and systematically addressing key risks � This disciplined approach preserves 
stakeholder interests and supports enduring business resilience � Our comprehensive 
framework positions us to respond strategically, advancing both continuity and 
managed expansion �Risk Mitigation Measures
Intensifying Market 
Competition yImprove product differentiation through innovation and value-added solutions.
 yExpand distribution reach, deepen customer engagement and build brand equity across 
categories.
 yFocus on operational efficiency and cost optimisation to maintain competitiveness.
Raw Material Price 
Volatility yCultivate strategic sourcing relationships, diversify sourcing channels and maintain long-
term supplier agreements. 
 yLeverage backward integration and improve inventory planning for critical materials. 
 yActively monitor market shifts and implement timely price interventions.
Supply Chain 
Disruptions yMaintain a diversified supplier base across geographies.
 yEnhance supply chain visibility and risk monitoring mechanism.
 yMaintain strategic inventory reserves and strengthen local sourcing capabilities wherever 
feasible.
Technology and 
Innovation Risk yInvest in focused research and development with an emphasis on emerging 
technologies. 
 yEstablish collaborations with industry specialists and academic partners. 
 ySystematically upgrade processes and manufacturing capabilities.
Environmental and 
Social Responsibility yIntegrate sustainability considerations into operations, manufacturing and product 
development.  
 yLead with proactive community engagement and responsible corporate initiatives. 
 yAdhere rigorously to environmental standards and industry norms.
Talent Acquisition 
and Retention yCultivate high performance and inclusive work environment.
 yInvest in employee development, leadership pipelines and capability building initiatives. 
 yMaintain competitive compensation and benefits structure.
 yDevelop structured training and career growth programmes. Cultivate an inclusive and 
supportive organisational culture. 
Capacity Expansion 
and Project Execution 
Risk yAdopt structured project management and governance processes for new facilities and 
capacity additions. 
 yMonitor project milestones closely to ensure timely execution, cost discipline and 
operational readiness.
Product Quality and 
Safety yMaintain stringent quality management systems, testing protocols and certification 
standards.
 yConduct periodic audits and continuously strengthen quality assurance process across 
manufacturing facilities. 
Intellectual Property 
Protection yUndertake comprehensive due diligence prior to product development.
 yImplement stringent intellectual property safeguards and regular monitoring. 
 yEnsure all rights, licenses, and agreements are meticulously in order.
Cybersecurity and 
Data Privacy yEstablish and maintain advanced cybersecurity infrastructure, monitoring systems and 
incident response capabilities. 
 yConduct regular security audits, employee awareness programmes and data protection 
reviews. 
 yDeliver regular employee training on data protection and privacy protocols.
Regulatory and 
Compliance Risk yMaintain robust compliance frameworks and governance mechanisms. 
 yContinuously monitor regulatory developments and implement timely corrective actions 
to ensure adherence to applicable laws, standards and industry requirements.RISK MANAGEMENT FRAMEWORK

and Reporting

GLOBAL ECONOMY 
The global economy grew at a stable 3.3% in 2025 
amidst another year of complex geopolitical and 
trade environment. Advanced economies grew 1.8% 
with United States recording strong 2.8% growth 
driven by resilient domestic demand and continued 
investments in AI and digital technologies. The euro 
area expanded by 0.8%, reflecting weaker industrial 
activity and cautious consumer spending.
Emerging markets grew by 4.3% supported by 
domestic consumption, infrastructure investments, 
and favourable demographic trends. India continued 
to be the fastest growing large economy. 
The World Economic Outlook projects the world 
economy to grow 3.1% in 2026. However, the outlook 
reflects heightened uncertainty arising from trade 
tensions, evolving tariff regimes, and geopolitical 
developments, including the escalation of conflict in 
the Middle East, that has renewed concerns around 
energy prices, shipping routes and global inflation. INDIAN ECONOMY 
According to the National Statistical Institute, the 
Indian economy grew 7.7% in FY 26, higher than the 
7.1% growth registered last fiscal. Growth remained 
broad based despite an increasingly uncertain 
global environment, supported by strong domestic 
demand, sustained public expenditure spending, 
improving private investment activity and recovery in 
rural consumption. 
Economic activity was supported by robust 
performance across key sectors. Construction 
remained one of the strongest contributors to 
growth, supported by sustained infrastructure 
spending, housing activity and urban development 
projects. Manufacturing growth improved from 
8.9% in FY 25 to 9.4% in FY 26, reflecting improved 
capacity utilisation, domestic demand and ongoing 
investment in production capabilities. Agriculture, 
forestry and fishery 4.6% while financial, real estate 
and professional services expanded 7.2%, underlining 
the continued strength of India’s service economy. 
High frequency indicators continued to reflect 
underlying resilience of the economy. Gross GST 
collections increased 8.3% to reach over ₹ 22.27 lakh 
crore.
The Reserve Bank of India predicts the Indian 
economy to grow by 6.6% for FY27. While global 
trade tensions, escalation of conflict in the middle 
East and likely El Nino impact on monsoon might 
create near term uncertainties, India’s structural 
growth drivers, including rapid urbanisation rising 
household incomes, infrastructure development, 
manufacturing expansion and increasing 
formalisation of the economy are expected to 
support sustained long-term growth. 
(Source - IMF, WEO April 2026)

MD&A (CONTD.)
INDIAN PLASTIC PIPES INDUSTRY  
The Indian plastic pipe industry has evolved from 
being a supplier of basic plumbing and irrigation 
products into a critical enabler of the country’s water, 
sanitation, housing and infrastructure ecosystem. 
Plastic piping systems today play an integral 
role across potable water distribution, sewage 
management, irrigation, industrial applications, 
drainage and fire protection networks. 
The industry continues to benefit from increasing 
replacement of conventional materials such as 
galvanised iron, concrete and metal-based systems 
with advanced polymer solutions that offer superior 
durability, corrosion resistance, lower lifecycle costs 
and ease of installation. Simultaneously, rising quality 
standards, stricter BIS compliance requirements and 
increasing customer preference for branded products 
have accelerated the shift from unorganised to 
organized manufacturers. 
According to industry estimates the Indian plastic 
pipe market size reached 3.08 Million Tons in 
2025 and is projected to reach 5.62 Million Tons 
by 2034, exhibiting a CAGR of 6.59% during the 
forecast period 2026-2034. The industry's growth is 
increasingly being shaped by large scale investments 
in water infrastructure, urbanisation and housing 
development. INDIAN ADHESIVES AND SEALANTS 
INDUSTRY 
The Indian adhesives and sealant industry serves as a 
facilitator across construction, consumer goods and 
industrial manufacturing landscapes. The industry 
covers applications such as woodworking, flexible 
packaging, footwear, tile grouts, epoxy systems, 
construction chemical products and specialised 
engineering bonding in automotive assembly and 
electronics.
According to the IMARC group, the domestic adhesive 
market stands at USD 2.55 bn in 2025 and is 
expected to reach USD 4.51 billion by 2034. 
FY26 Performance
The industry delivered a healthy growth during FY 
26, supported by strong demand from construction, 
infrastructure, packaging and manufacturing end 
markets. While volatility in crude oil linked raw 
materials and chemical intermediaries influenced 
input costs during the parts of the year, underlying 
demand remained strong across most segments. FY26 Performance and Trends
FY 26 was characterised by strong underlying 
demand across housing, infrastructure, and water 
management applications, despite significant volatility 
in raw material markets during the year.
Demand was supported by continued execution of 
government programmes focused on water access, 
sanitation, and housing. As of June 2026, over 15.8 
crore rural households had been provided tap-water 
connections under the Jal Jeevan Mission, compared 
to approximately 3.2 crore households at the launch 
of the programme in 2019. The Union Budget FY26 
further reinforced this momentum with an allocation 
of ₹67,000 crore towards the Jal Jeevan Mission and 
₹70,000+ crore towards Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana.
The operating environment witnessed two distinct 
phases during the year. For much of FY 26, industry 
participants operated in a low-price PVC environment 
following an extended period of global oversupply and 
weak feedstock pricing. However, during the latter 
part of the year, geopolitical tensions in the Middle 
East, supply chain disruptions and changes in the 
global trade flows led to sharp increase in PVC resin 
prices, triggering widespread channel restocking and 
improving realisations across the industry.
At the same time, organised manufacturers continued 
to gain market share as their stronger balance sheets, 
wider distribution reach and superior inventory 
management capabilities enabled them to navigate 
raw material volatility more effectively than smaller 
regional players.
Growth Drivers and Structural Tailwinds
The long-term structural outlook of the Indian plastic 
pipe industry continues to be supported by multiple 
structural growth drivers. 
Water Infrastructure and Urbanisation
India’s growing  focus on water security, wastewater 
management and urban infrastructure remains 
one of the largest demand catalyst for the industry. 
Government programmes such as the Jal Jeevan 
Mission, AMRUT 2.0, PM Awas Yojana, PM Krishi 
Sinchai Yojana continue to expand country’s water 
and sanitation infrastructure.
The Indian housing market is witnessing gradual 
shift towards premium and mid-premium residential 
developments. This trend is driving higher adoption 
of value-added products such as CPVC plumbing 
systems, acoustic drainage solutions, fire protection 
piping systems and advanced water management 
technologies.
India continues to depend on imports for a significant 
portion of its PVC and CPVC resin requirements. 
However, substantial investments in domestic resin 
manufacturing and backward integration projects are 
underway across the industry. These investments are 
expected to improve supply security, reduce import 
dependence and support long-term margin stability 
and will reduce working capital requirement.Scheme FY27

Outlook
The outlook for Indian plastic pipe industry 
remains favourable, supported by sustained 
investments in water infrastructure, housing 
and urban development. Industry volumes are 
expected to grow by 7.5%-8% with organised 
players likely to outperform on the back of 
stronger brands, wider distribution networks 
and certified product portfolios. The next 
phase of growth is expected to be driven by 
increasing penetration of specialised and value-
added categories such as CPVC, HDPE, PEX, 
fire protection systems and advanced drainage 
solutions. India's focus on water infrastructure, 
urban development and housing quality 
continues to intensify, organised manufacturers 
with strong brands, broad product portfolios 
and integrated manufacturing capabilities are 
expected to be key beneficiaries of the sector's 
long-term growth trajectory.

Market conditions improved in the second half of 
the year, supported by housing completions, festive 
demand, and stronger rural consumption, driving 
volume growth in decorative paints, particularly 
across Tier II and Tier III markets. Continued 
premiumisation and expansion of organised 
distribution also supported demand recovery. 
However, lower realisations and downtrading within 
product categories weighed on revenue growth, 
with the decorative segment experiencing limited 
pricing power throughout the year.  
 
Industrial coatings remained relatively resilient, 
supported by healthy demand from the automotive, 
infrastructure, engineering, and capital goods 
sectors. Sustained activity across manufacturing and 
public infrastructure projects enabled mid-single-
digit growth in segments such as protective and 
powder coatings, despite the challenging pricing 
environment.
Growth Drivers 
India's construction sector grew by 8.4% in FY 26. 
The expanding housing stock remains one of the 
prime growth drivers for the paints industry. The 
infrastructure build up also generates demand for 
protective coatings, waterproofing systems and 
specialised industrial applications.
Consumer preferences for premium and 
differentiated products are expected to increase 
demand for emulsions, textured finishes, waterproof 
coatings and specialised surface solutions, growing 
market for higher value product categories.
As building standards evolve and awareness of 
preventive maintenance increases, demand for 
waterproofing systems and surface protection 
products is growing faster than traditional paint 
categories, expanding the industry's addressable 
market.Demand Drivers
Construction and infrastructure emerged as key 
growth drivers. Increased activity across housing, 
urban infrastructure, transportation networks 
and industrial projects supported demand for 
waterproofing solutions, tile adhesives, repair mortars, 
grouts and construction sealants. As construction 
quality continues to improve, the use of specialised 
construction chemicals is becoming increasingly 
mainstream across residential and commercial 
projects.
Packaging is expected to benefit from continued 
expansion of organised retail, e-commerce, quick 
commerce and flexible packaging. Demand for hot 
melt adhesives, pressure sensitive adhesives and 
laminating solution remains robust. 
India’s strong momentum in manufacturing 
including automotive, appliances, electronics and 
engineered products is creating incremental demand 
for industrial adhesives and specialised bonding 
solutions. 
The industry is gradually moving to specialised and 
technology driven formulations including water based 
adhesives, polyurethane systems, hybrid sealants 
and low-VOC products are gaining prominence 
due to evolving environmental standards and 
customer preferences. These categories typically offer 
higher performance characteristics and improved 
profitability relative to conventional products.INDIAN SANITARYWARE AND 
BATHWARE INDUSTRY
The Indian sanitaryware and bathware industry has 
significantly evolved over the last decade, moving 
beyond basic utility products towards premium, 
design-led and water efficient solutions. Rising 
urbanisation, increasing homeownership, growing 
disposable incomes and changing consumer 
aspirations continue to support long-term demand. 
Market size: The India bath fittings market size is 
expected to increase from USD 11.49 billion in 2025 
to USD 12.36 billion in 2026 and reach USD 17.97 
billion by 2031, growing at a CAGR of 7.8% over 2026-
2031.
FY26 Performance
FY26 marked a year of recovery for the industry 
after witnessing subdued demand for FY 25. While 
retail demand remained relatively cautious during 
the first half of the year, demand from residential 
developments, commercial real estate and hospitality 
projects provided support to the industry volumes. 
Market conditions improved considerably during the 
second half as housing completions accelerated and 
demand from premium and mid premium residential 
projects emerged.  INDIAN PAINTS INDUSTRY
The Indian paints industry remains one of the fastest-
growing segments within the country's building 
materials and specialty chemicals landscape. The 
industry has steadily evolved beyond traditional 
decorative paints to include waterproofing 
solutions, protective coatings, and specialised 
surface technologies, supported by urbanisation, 
infrastructure development, and rising demand for 
premium products.  
 
According to IBEF, the Indian paints and coatings 
industry was valued at approximately ₹93,000 crore 
in 2025 and is projected to reach ₹1.46 lakh crore 
by 2030. Decorative paints account for nearly three-
fourths of industry demand, while industrial coatings 
cater to sectors such as automotive, infrastructure, 
engineering, and manufacturing. 
FY26 Performance
FY26 witnessed a gradual improvement in demand 
conditions, especially in the latter half of the year, 
following a relatively subdued first half. The operating 
environment remained highly competitive as both 
established players and new entrants intensified 
investments in channel expansion, dealer incentives 
and brand-building initiatives. While industry pricing 
power remained constrained despite stable demand 
and favourable input costs, Astral continued to 
outperform the broader industry through its strong 
brand equity, extensive distribution network, product 
innovation and execution capabilities. The Company 
delivered healthy growth, strengthened its market 
position and continued to gain market share across 
key categories despite the challenging competitive 
landscape.  MD&A (CONTD.)
Outlook
Indian adhesives and sealants market is 
expected to register volume growth of  ~8-
10% in FY27, driven by construction activity, 
renovation demand, and growing rural 
consumption. The next phase of industry 
growth is expected to be led by waterproofing 
systems, tile-fixing solutions, repair and 
rehabilitation products, industrial bonding 
technologies and sustainable formulations. 
Organised players with strong brands, technical 
capabilities, diversified product portfolios and 
extensive distribution networks are expected 
to be key beneficiaries of these trends. The UK 
adhesives market is expected to remain under 
macroeconomic pressure, though early signs of 
stabilisation and now it is visible also however 
export opportunities across the Middle East, 
Africa, and Europe remain strong.Outlook
While competitive intensity remains elevated, 
the sector’s structural growth drivers remain 
firmly intact. The next phase of industry 
growth is likely to be characterised by deeper 
penetration into tier II and tier III cities, greater 
adoption of specialised and sustainable 
products and increasing convergence between 
decorative coatings, waterproofing solutions and 
construction chemicals.

PERFORMANCE REVIEWGovernment initiatives aimed at improving urban

categories. 
Growth Drivers
Along with rising urbanisation and subsequent 
increase in housing and premiumisation of lifestyle, 
water efficiency and sustainability awareness is another 
growth factor. This is leading to trends like adoption 
of water efficient products such as dual flush systems, 
low flow faucets, aerators and sensor-based fittings. 
Green building standards and sustainability initiatives 
are expected to further support demand for resource 
efficient bathroom solutions in the years ahead.
Investments in hotels, hospitals, educational 
institutions, airports and commercial developments 
continue to create significant demand for 
sanitaryware and bath fittings. Growing infrastructure 
investments and urban development projects are 
expected to provide additional opportunities for 
organised industry participants.ABOUT THE COMPANY
Established in 1996, Astral Limited has evolved 
from a pioneering pipes and plumbing solutions 
company into one of India’s leading building materials 
enterprises. Over nearly three decades, the company 
has consistently expanded its product portfolio, 
manufacturing footprint and distribution reach, 
building a diversified platform that serves the evolving 
needs of residential, commercial, industrial and 
infrastructure markets. 
Today, Astral operates across nine high-growth 
business categories consisting of pipes and 
fittings, water tanks, bathware, specialised valves, 
infrastructure solutions, specialised fittings, adhesives 
and sealants, paints and construction chemicals. This 
diversified portfolio enables the company to offer 
comprehensive solutions across the building material 
value chain while reducing dependence on any one 
product category.
Astral operates 21 manufacturing units across 
India, the United Kingdom, and the United States, 
supported by a total production capacity of 
approximately 5,97,089 metric tonnes per annum. 
The company strengthened its production capabilities 
during the year through the commissioning of Kanpur 
facility and continued progress on its upcoming 
Khambhat manufacturing unit. 
Astral’s market reach is enabled by a robust 
distribution network comprising over 2.7 lakh dealers 
and more than 3,900 distributors, ensuring product 
availability across urban and rural markets alike. The 
company also maintains a growing presence in export 
markets, supplying to over 40 countries worldwide.
Innovation remains central to Astral’s growth 
philosophy. Over the years, the company has 
consistently expanded into adjacent categories 
through product development, strategic acquisitions 
and portfolio diversification. Recent initiatives include 
the launch of new plumbing and industrial piping 
solutions, expansion of the bathware portfolio, 
strengthening of adhesive business through entry into 
modern trade and DIY solutions along with continued 
scale up of Astral Paints and construction chemicals. 
Astral's strong brands, extensive distribution 
capabilities, manufacturing excellence and customer-
centric approach have helped it build leadership 
positions across multiple categories. Supported by 
a workforce of more than 9,500 employees, the 
Company continues to focus on sustainable growth, 
operational excellence and long-term value creation 
for all stakeholders.Outlook
The outlook for the Indian sanitaryware and 
bath industry remains favourable. The industry 
is also expected to benefit from increasing 
replacement demand as consumers upgrade 
existing bathrooms with modern, aesthetically 
designed and technologically advanced 
products. As consumer preferences continue 
to evolve towards premium, design-led and 
sustainable solutions, organised players with 
strong brands, integrated product portfolios 
and extensive distribution networks are 
expected to be well positioned to capture 
future growth opportunities.MD&A (CONTD.)
Pipes and fittings segment
The industry demand for this segment remained mixed 
amid continued polymer price volatility and a cautious 
constructive environment. Despite these challenges, 
Astral delivered healthy volume growth of 11.5%  
supported by product innovation, network expansion and 
capacity augmentation. The commissioning of facilities 
in Hyderabad and Kanpur improved regional servicing 
capabilities, while value added products across plumbing, 
drainage, fire protection and infrastructure applications 
continued to gain traction. Astral also expanded its export 
footprint, serving 40 countries and reporting strong 
growth in export volumes during the year. 
OUTLOOK
Astral expects the demand to be supported by continued 
investments in housing, water infrastructure and urban 
development. The company expects polymer prices 
to remain elevated after a year of sharp volatility. The 
company’s expanding manufacturing footprint, growing 
export presence and increasing contribution from 
premium products such as OPVC, PEX, FirePro and 
specialised drainage system position it well to capture 
emerging opportunities across both domestic and 
international markets. 
Paints Business
Astral expanded its distribution footprint across western 
India while consolidating its presence in the southern 
markets. The company invested in channel development, 
branding and product positioning and delivered healthy 
growth of 23.0% and creating a stronger platform for 
future growth and improved operation performance. 
OUTLOOK
With the initial phase of market development largely 
established, the company’s focus is now on accelerating 
distribution expansion, improving product mix and 
enhancing operational efficiencies. Astral remains focused 
on building a scalable paints platform through disciplined 
market expansion, improving product mix and continued 
investments in brand visibility and dealer engagement. Adhesives and sealants business
The adhesives and sealants business registered another 
year of healthy growth of 12.6% , driven by deeper 
market penetration, portfolio expansion and continued 
investments in brand building. Astral enhanced its 
presence across construction chemicals, waterproofing 
and specialised adhesives, while advancing backward 
integration initiatives and expanding its reach through 
modern trade and digital channels. International 
operations also showed resilience, with UK business 
returning to profitability and US business delivering 
encouraging growth. Further, Astral has also started 
office in Dubai. This will also add growth trajectory in 
export market.  
OUTLOOK
This business is expected to benefit from increasing 
demand for construction chemicals, waterproofing 
solutions and specialised industrial adhesives. Ongoing 
investment in capacity, product development and 
distribution expansion are expected to strengthen 
market reach and operational efficiencies. Astral also 
sees significant long term potential from backward 
integration initiatives and growing participation in 
export markets. 
Bathware Business
The bathware business maintained strong momentum, 
supported by growing brand acceptance, an expanding 
distribution network and increasing participation in 
residential and commercial projects, The company 
broadened its premium product portfolio and 
strengthened its product pipeline, while leveraging 
Astral’s established ecosystem to deepen market 
penetration. The segment continued to demonstrate 
encouraging growth of 27.3% and remains an 
important component of Astral’s long-term building 
materials strategy. 
OUTLOOK
The Company expects the bathware business to benefit 
from rising premiumisation, increasing adoption of 
branded products and growing demand for integrated 
bathroom solutions. Continued investments in 
product innovation, dealer development and project 
engagement are expected to support scale-up and 
improve profitability over the medium term.

INTERNAL CONTROL SYSTEMS  
AND THEIR ADEQUACY
Astral has established a comprehensive internal 
control framework designed to ensure the orderly 
conduct of its business, safeguard its assets, and 
maintain the integrity of its financial reporting. These 
controls extend across all functions of the Company 
and are structured to promote operational efficiency, 
ensure reliable accounting and financial information, 
and support compliance with applicable legal and 
regulatory requirements.
The internal control environment is continually 
reviewed and enhanced to reflect evolving business 
requirements and industry standards. Internal audit 
processes are conducted independently and regularly, 
with oversight from the Internal Auditor, and findings 
are reviewed by the Audit Committee of the Board. 
The Committee monitors the implementation of 
recommendations and ensures that corrective actions 
are taken in a timely manner.
Independent statutory auditors have, as part of their 
audit procedures, confirmed the adequacy of internal 
financial controls over reporting. This structured and 
evolving system of internal controls reflects Astral’s 
continued commitment to transparency, good 
governance, and the long-term confidence of all 
stakeholders.
HUMAN RESOURCES
Astral recognises that its people are central to its 
success and continues to invest in building a strong, 
capable, and engaged workforce. The Company’s 
human resource strategy is focused on creating a 
work environment that encourages performance, 
growth, and professional development across all levels 
of the organisation.
As on March 31, 2026 Astral’s workforce stood at 
over 9500 employees, including contract personnel, 
deployed across multiple locations and business 
verticals. This diverse and dedicated team continues 
to be a key driver of operational performance and 
innovation across the Company’s businesses.KEY FINANCIAL RATIOS
Astral places strong emphasis on employee learning 
and development, leadership building, and workforce 
well-being. Structured training programmes, skill 
enhancement initiatives, and performance-linked 
development pathways are integral to the HR 
approach. The Company also maintains robust 
HR policies that align with its core values and are 
designed to promote fairness, inclusivity, and 
professional growth. Going forward, Astral remains 
committed to strengthening its talent base and 
fostering a culture of accountability, collaboration, 
and continuous learning—recognising that sustained 
organisational success is built on the strength and 
commitment of its people.

2. DIVIDEND
During the year under review, the Board of Directors declared 
and paid Interim Dividend of ` 1.50/- (150%) per equity share. 
Further your directors have recommended a Final Dividend of 
` 2.50/- (250%) per equity share for the financial year ended 
March 31, 2026 subject to approval of shareholders in the 
ensuing Annual General Meeting. With the above, the total 
dividend for the year under review would be ` 4.00/- (400%) 
per equity share. Interim Dividend Paid for the FY 2025-2026 
along with the Final Dividend, if approved in the ensuing 
Annual General Meeting shall be about ` 1,074 million. 
The dividend recommended is in accordance with the 
Company’s policy on dividend distribution. The said 
policy is available on the website of the Company as can 
be accessed at https:/ /www.astralltd.com/wp-content/
uploads/2023/01/1668401922_policy_on_dividend_
distribution.pdf .

No amount is proposed to be transferred to the reserves 
during the year under review.
4. CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL AND 
OPERATIONAL PERFORMANCE
• Consolidated Revenue from Operations has increased 
by 12.62% from ` 58,324 million to ` 65,686 million.
• Consolidated EBITDA has increased by 12.36% from 
` 9,872 million to `11,092 million.
• Consolidated Profit Before Tax (before exceptional 
items) has increased by 7.22% from ` 7,025 million to 
` 7,532 million.
• Consolidated Profit After Tax has increased by 3.04% 
from ` 5,189 million to ` 5,347 million.
5. OPERATIONAL PERFORMANCE AND 
CAPITAL EXPENDITURE
• During the year under review, your Company has 
increased its installed capacity of plumbing business 
by 9.34% from 3,81,957 MT to 4,17,645 MT and Sales 
Volume has increased from 2,27,090 MT to 2,63,026 MT.
• During the year under review, your Company has 
incurred capital expenditure to the tune of ` 3,284 
million towards plant & machineries, factory building and 
other capital expenditure.
6. ACQUISITIONS AND INCREASE IN 
SHAREHOLDING IN SUBSIDIARIES
During the financial year ended March 31, 2026, the 
Company undertook strategic acquisitions and increased its 
ownership in certain subsidiaries with a view to strengthening 
its business portfolio, enhancing operational efficiencies, and 
creating long-term value for stakeholders.
The Board of Directors of the company on April 17, 2025, 
approved the acquisition of 100% equity shares of Al-Aziz Plastics Private Limited, which is expected to strengthen the 
Company's presence in the plastic pipes and fittings segment 
through product portfolio diversification and additional 
manufacturing capacities. Further, on August 11, 2025, the 
Board of Directors of the Company approved the acquisition 
of 80% equity shares of Nexelon Chem Private Limited. This 
acquisition marks a strategic backward integration initiative, 
enabling the Company to manufacture CPVC resin, a key 
raw material used in its operations, thereby supporting 
cost optimization, supply chain efficiency, and margin 
enhancement.
During the year under review, the Board of Directors, at 
its meetings held on September 4, 2025 and September 
10, 2025, approved the acquisition of the remaining 
5% equity stake in Seal It Services Limited, UK and 20% 
equity stake in Astral Chemie Limited (formerly known as 
Astral Coatings Private Limited), respectively. Pursuant 
to these acquisitions, both entities became wholly owned 
subsidiaries of the Company.
These acquisitions and investments are aligned with the 
Company's long-term growth strategy and are expected to 
enhance business synergies, strengthen market positioning, 
improve operational integration, and contribute to sustainable 
value creation for all stakeholders.
7. SUBSIDIARY/JOINT VENTURE 
COMPANIES
As at March 31, 2026, your Company has 5 (Five) direct 
subsidiaries namely Seal It Services Limited (UK), Astral 
Foundation, Astral Chemie Limited (formerly known as Astral

and Nexelon Chem Private Limited and 2 (Two) step down

18. STATE OF COMPANY AFFAIRS
During the financial year 2025-26, Astral Limited continued 
to strengthen its position as one of India's leading 
manufacturers of building materials and plumbing solutions. 
The Company maintained its leadership in the CPVC and PVC 
piping systems segment while further expanding its presence 
across adhesives, sealants, paints, water storage solutions, 
faucets, sanitaryware and allied building material products. 
The Company's extensive distribution network, strong brand 
equity, continuous focus on innovation and customer-centric 
approach enabled it to strengthen its market presence across 
domestic and international markets.
The Company remains focused on innovation-led growth and 
continues to invest in research and development to deliver 
technologically advanced, high-quality and sustainable 
products. Its dedicated R&D initiatives support new product 
development, process improvements, quality enhancement, 
import substitution and cost optimization across business 
segments. The Company's emphasis on innovation enables 
it to address evolving customer requirements, strengthen its 
competitive advantage and create differentiated offerings 
across its product portfolio.
With a diversified product portfolio, strong distribution reach, 
continuous investments in capacity expansion, research 
and development, innovation and strategic acquisitions, 
the Company remains well positioned to capitalize 
on opportunities arising from growth in the housing, 
infrastructure and construction sectors. Astral continues 
to focus on sustainable growth, operational excellence and 
long-term value creation for all its stakeholders.
19. MATERIAL CHANGES AND 
COMMITMENT AFFECTING FINANCIAL

There have been no material changes or commitments that 
have affected the financial position of the Company between 
March 31, 2026 and the date of this Report.

monitoring of internal risk factors. Your Company has an 
Internal Financial Control System commensurate with the 
size, scale and complexity of its operations. Your Company 
has adopted proper system of Internal Control and Risk

protected against loss from unauthorized use or disposition

1. Management’s Responsibility
 Management is responsible for the maintenance of the Secretarial records and for the preparation and filing of forms,

2. Secretarial Auditor’s Responsibility
 Secretarial Audit is a process of verification of records and documents on sample or test basis. My responsibility is to express 
an opinion on the secretarial compliances of certain laws by the Company on the basis of my audit. The audit practices 
and processes have been followed as deemed appropriate to provide reasonable assurance about the correctness of the 
records and the confirmation of compliance. My audit process has involved verification of records and dependence on 
Management representation and my opinion is based thereupon.
3. Conduct of Company’s Affairs
 The Secretarial Audit Report is neither an assurance as to the future viability of the Company nor of the efficacy or 
effectiveness with which the management has conducted the affairs of the company.

EARNINGS AND OUTGO:
(Information as required under Section 134 of the Companies Act, 2013 read with the Companies (Accounts) Rules, 2014 is set 
out hereunder)
A. CONSERVATION OF ENERGY:
(i) Steps taken for conservation of energy:
Energy conservation continues to be the key focus area of 
your Company. The Company is making continuous effort 
for energy conservation. Effective measures have been 
taken to monitor consumption of energy during the process 
of manufacture. Continuous monitoring and awareness 
amongst employees have helped to avoid wastage of energy. 
The Company has continued taking following steps for 
conservation of energy during FY 2025-26:
- About 1,47,9,074 kWh energy saved in Pipe Segment, 
13,352 kWh energy saved in Bathware and 64080.36 
kWh energy saved in Adhesive Segment by Modification 
of Machineries set up, replacement of Conventional 
Lights with LED Lights, Changing Energy Efficient 
equipment’s, Cooling Tower, Air Cooled Chillers etc. 
(ii) Steps taken by the Company for utilizing 
alternate sources of energy:
Solar power roof top panel Installed for Santej and total 
9,19,138 kWh generated and utilized, Dholka total 11,52,851 
kWh generated and utilized, Ghiloth total 14,75,092 kWh 
generated and utilized, Sangli total 6,62,627 kWh generated 
and utilized, Hosur total 12,84,956 kWh generated and 
utilized, Santej Adhesive Plant total 2,55,894 kWh generated 
and utilized & Rania Adhesive Plant total 1,14,065 kWh 
generated and utilized. 
Also procured green power from Wind mills installed at Virvav 
village near Morbi to get 2.92 MW (contracted capacity) for 
Santej Plant and 2.25 MW (contracted capacity) for Dholka 
Plant under third party open access arrangements. During 
the current financial year, the Company has entered into an 
agreement for procuring Wind-Solar Hybrid green power 
installed at near Bhavnagar to get 2.60 MW (contracted 
capacity) for Santej Plant and 1.90 MW (contracted capacity) 
for Dholka Plant under third party open access arrangements.
Wind Energy consumed at Santej Plant is total 69,35,571 
kWh and at Dholka is total 42,86,204 kWh, Hybrid (Wind and Solar) Energy consumed at Santej Plant is total 90,64,325 
kWh and at Dholka is total 69,71,837 kWh.
During the current year, Astral Limited has entered into a 
Share Subscription and Shareholders’ Agreement (SSSA) 
with Vena Energy Sustainable Power Private Limited for 
procurement of 7 MW renewable power from a hybrid solar-
wind captive power plant in Gujarat for its Santej and Dholka 
facilities. The project is expected to commence power supply 
from Q2 of FY 2026–27 and marks a significant step in Astral’s 
sustainability and clean energy transition journey
B. TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION:
(i) Efforts made towards technology 
absorption:
Your Company lays considerable emphasis on quality 
maintenance and product enhancement. The Company is 
continuously trying to develop more and more products in its 
R & D Center. During the year under review, your Company 
has spent ` 16.59 Millions for its ultramodern R & D center at 
its Plants and the Company now is in a position to carry out a 
lot of R & D activities in-house.
More and more emphasis has been given to the automation 
process and Company has selected packaging operation as 
an area of immediate automation. The Company has invested 
significant amount of resources for atomization of pipe and 
fitting operations. 
(ii) The benefits derived like product 
improvement, cost reduction, product 
development or import substitution:
Your Company’s efforts in quality, maintenance and product 
enhancement have resulted in better quality products at a 
low cost of production.

Corporate Governance Report
1. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE PHILOSOPHY
Your Company believes in adopting the best corporate governance practices, based on the following principles in order to 
maintain transparency, accountability and ethics:
• Recognition of the respective roles and responsibilities of the management;
• Independent verification and assured integrity of financial reporting;
• Protection of Shareholders’ right and priority for investor relations; and
• Timely and accurate disclosure on all material matters concerning operations and performance of your Company.
Keeping the above in mind, your Company is fully committed to conduct its affairs in a fair and transparent manner and to 
enhance long-term shareholder value while complying with the applicable laws, rules, regulations and governance standards. 
The Company is in compliance with all applicable corporate governance requirements prescribed under the Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 ("SEBI Listing Regulations").

payable to the Managing Director/Whole Time Director. The

component consisting of salary and perquisites in accordance 
with Company’s policy and a profit linked incentive.
Non – Executive Directors:
Non-Executive Directors are paid sitting fees for attending 
the Board and Committee meetings, plus the reimbursement 
of actual expense directly related to the travel and out-of-
pocket expenses, if any, incurred by them.

Performance Evaluation:
Pursuant to the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and SEBI Listing Regulations, the Committee has carried out the annual 
performance evaluation of Directors and Key Managerial Personnel. The Board of Directors also carried out annual performance 
evaluation of Independent Directors and Committees of the Board. Performance evaluation was carried out based on approved 
criteria such as adherence to ethical standards and code of conduct, constructive participation in board meetings, implementing 
good corporate governance practices etc. The Directors expressed their satisfaction with the evaluation process.
The independent directors also held separate meeting to review the performance of Non- Independent Directors and Board as 
whole, reviewed the performance of Chairman of the Company, taking into account the views of Executive Directors and Non-
Executive Directors and assessed the quality, quantity and timeliness of flow of information between the Company Management 
and the Board.
(iv) Risk Management Committee:
Regulation 21 of the SEBI Listing Regulations mandates top 1000 listed entities, determined on the basis of market capitalization 
as at the end of the immediate previous financial year, to constitute a Risk Management Committee (‘RMC’) to assist the Board 
of Directors in overseeing the Company’s risk management processes and controls. The RMC seeks to minimize adverse impact 
on the business objectives and enhance stakeholder value.
Composition, meetings and attendance:
The Risk Management Committee of your Company has been constituted pursuant to Regulation 21 of the SEBI Listing 
Regulations. The Chairman of the Committee is the member of the Board.
During the Financial Year 2025-26, the Committee met 2 (Two) times on May 21, 2025 and November 05, 2025.
The composition of the Risk Management Committee as on March 31, 2026 and the attendance of the members in the meetings

Terms of Reference:
The terms of reference of the Risk Management Committee, inter alia, are as under:
• To formulate a detailed risk management policy which shall include:
a) A framework for identification of internal and external risks specifically faced by the listed entity, in particular including 
financial, operational, sectoral, sustainability (particularly, ESG related risks), information, cyber security risks or any 
other risk as may be determined by the Committee.

about the risk assessment and minimization procedures 
adopted by the Company. The Audit Committee of the Board 
is also regularly informed about the business risks and the 
steps taken to mitigate the same. The implementation of the

process and the Board members are periodically informed of 
the status.
(e) Familiarization Program of Independent 
Directors:
The Board familiarization program comprises of the following:
• Induction program for new Independent Directors;
• Presentation on business and functional issues;
• Updating of business, branding, corporate governance, 
regulatory developments and investor relations matters.
All new Independent Directors are taken through a detailed 
induction and familiarization program when they join the 
Board of your Company. The induction program is an 
exhaustive one that covers the history and culture of your 
company, background of the Company and its growth over 
the decades, various milestones in the Company’s existence 
since its incorporation, the present structure and an overview 
of the businesses and functions.Independent Directors are familiarized with their roles, rights 
and responsibilities in the Company as well as with the nature 
of industry and business model of the Company by providing 
various presentations at Board/Committee meetings 
from time to time. These presentations provide a good 
understanding of the business to the Independent Directors 
which covers various functions of the Company and also 
an opportunity for the Board to interact with the next level 
of management. There are opportunities for Independent 
Directors to interact amongst themselves.
Apart from the above, the Directors are also given an update 
on the environmental and social impact of the business, 
branding, corporate governance, regulatory developments 
and investor relations matters.
The details of the Familiarization programmes can be 
accessed on the web link https:/ /www.astralltd.com/wp-
content/uploads/2026/03/Familiarisation-Programme-of-
Independent-Directors_DJSI-Updated.pdf

(h) Transfer to Investor Education and 
Protection Fund (IEPF):
In terms of the Section 124 of the Companies Act, 2013, 
the amount that remained unclaimed for a period of seven 
years is required to be transferred to the Investor Education 
and Protection Fund (IEPF) administered by the Central 
Government.During the year under review, the unclaimed dividend amount 
of ` 23,988/- for the year 2017-18 (Final) & and `17,992/- 
for the year 2018-19 (Interim) was transferred to the IEPF 
established by the Central Government under applicable 
provisions of the Companies Act.
In terms of Section 124(6) of the Act read with Investor 
Education and Protection Fund Authority (Accounting, 
Auditing, Transfer and Refund) Rules, 2016, the Company has 
transferred the shares in respect of which the dividend has not 
been claimed for a period of seven years or more to the demat 
account of IEPF Authority. The Company had communicated 
to all the concerned shareholders individually, whose shares 
were liable to be transferred to IEPF. The Company had 
also given newspaper advertisements, before such transfer 
in favour of IEPF. The Company had also uploaded the 
details of such shareholders and shares transferred to IEPF 
on the website of the Company at https:/ /www.astralltd.
com/investors/. The Shareholders may note that both the 
unclaimed dividend and corresponding shares transferred 
to the IEPF Authority including all benefits accruing on 
such shares, if any, can be claimed back by them from IEPF 
Authority after following the procedure (i.e., an application 
in E-form No. IEPF-5) prescribed in the Rules. Shareholders 
may refer Rule 7 of the said Rules for Refund of shares/
dividend etc.
(i) Share Transfer System:
The Shares of Company are compulsorily traded in 
dematerialized form. The request for dematerialization of 
shares are also processed by the R&T agent within stipulated 
period and uploaded with the concerned Depositories. In 
terms of SEBI Listing Regulation, no transfer of shares in 
physical form can be lodged by the shareholders.

7. Statement by director responsible for the business responsibility report, highlighting ESG related challenges, targets 
and achievements (listed entity has flexibility regarding the placement of this disclosure)
At Astral, sustainability remains integral to the Company’s approach to responsible growth and long-term value creation. 
As a leading building materials company, Astral continues to advance infrastructure development and improve quality of 
life through innovative products while embedding environmental, social, and governance (ESG) considerations across 
its operations and value chain.
During FY 2025–26, Astral strengthened its sustainability journey through focused actions in resource efficiency, 
renewable energy, circularity, workplace safety, responsible governance, and community development. The Company 
achieved a 39% increase in renewable electricity generation and advanced its 7 MW clean energy initiative through 
solar and wind power generation, reinforcing its commitment to climate action. Astral also accelerated its sustainability 
management efforts through a digital ESG transformation programme that enhanced data collection, monitoring, and 
reporting capabilities.
Astral’s commitment to sustainable innovation is reflected in its portfolio of GRIHA-certified green building products, 
including Aquasafe, Silencio, Aquarius, Drain Pro, and Drainmaster. The Company further strengthened operational 
excellence through the implementation of ISO 9001:2015 Quality Management Systems at its Cuttack and Hyderabad 
manufacturing facilities.
Beyond business performance, Astral remains deeply committed to creating positive social impact. Through its CSR 
initiatives, the Company supported over 41.24 lakh beneficiaries during FY 2025–26, contributing to community 
development and inclusive growth. Guided by strong governance, ethical business practices, and stakeholder 
engagement, Astral continues to build a future-ready organisation that balances growth with environmental stewardship 
and social responsibility, creating sustainable value for all stakeholders.

4. Does the entity have an anti-corruption or anti-bribery policy? If yes, provide details in brief and 
if available, provide a web-link to the policy.
Yes, Astral has implemented a Policy on Anti-Corruption and Bribery to uphold the highest ethical and business standards in 
its operations. The policy establishes robust risk assessment procedures and internal control mechanisms by mandating ethical 
conduct, transparency in all business dealings, and a strict prohibition of bribery, corruption, misappropriation of funds, political 
contributions, embezzlement, and lobbying for personal or organisational gain, with violations subject to investigation and 
disciplinary action by a designated steering committee. It also provides a clear mechanism to deal with complaints related to 
bribery or corruption, ensuring that reported or identified concerns are addressed through structured inquiry and appropriate 
corrective measures, reflecting a zero-tolerance approach. Further, Astral ensures comprehensive coverage of anti-corruption 1. Percentage coverage by training and awareness programmes on any of the Principles during
